# QA review charter — the declared-schema substrate (ch13 re-derivation, phase P3, arc B)

You are a fresh-context quality reviewer on a CODE build. You have full
repo access. Read the repo yourself; do not trust any summary in this
charter over the bytes.

Repo: the working tree you are launched in. The BUILD's code tip is
`89e33abb`; this charter was committed after it, so the HEAD you see is
that commit or a later docs-only one. The bytes under review are pinned
by the hashes below, not by HEAD. Everything is repo-relative.

Review object — the build, at these bytes:

| File | Lines | sha256 (first 16) |
|---|---|---|
| `v3/src/definition/schema/vocabulary.ts` | 385 | `a370c52e3528c5fe` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/engine.ts` | 1131 | `628fd58755d4c34d` |
| `v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts` | 558 | `14ead799a7b38782` |

Cite your tree's HEAD in your verdict.

## 0. READ THIS FIRST — the time budget, and why it decides your output shape

**You have a HARD 20-minute wall-clock budget for this entire session.
It is enforced by an external process kill. A session that hits it
produces NO usable output — every finding you have not yet written out
is discarded.** An earlier session in this repo spent its whole budget
investigating, planned to write up at the end, and was killed mid-write-up.
All of its work was lost.

So the rule is: **write each finding out IN FULL, in the §4 format, the
moment you confirm it.** Do not accumulate findings for the end. Your
closing message should need to contain nothing but the counts, because
everything else is already written.

Budget shape: stop investigating at roughly the 12-minute mark and spend
the rest consolidating. Prefer **breadth at shallow depth**. If a check
looks like it will take more than about two minutes, write it down as
`unrun` with one sentence on what would settle it, and move on.

## 1. What the build is

The repo previously validated authored workflow-definition files with
two hand-written rule sets: a source-form walk over the YAML document
(`definition/validate.ts`, now deleted) and an admission rung over the
resulting value (`definition/admit.ts`). The two disagreed about which
rules ran on which channel, and the rules themselves lived as code.

This build replaces both with:

1. a VOCABULARY of declaration attributes, as types
   (`schema/vocabulary.ts`) — no logic;
2. a DECLARATION of the template format, as frozen data
   (`schema/templateFormat.ts`) — no logic, every node carrying a `tag`
   and the ratified contract rows it realizes in `rows`;
3. an ENGINE that consumes the declaration and emits findings
   (`schema/engine.ts`), run on either channel — a file (with its YAML
   document and source text) or a directly-constructed value;
4. a NORMALIZER (`schema/normalizer.ts`) that computes the admitted
   FORM — deliberately a separate machine, because a declaration says
   what is legal and does not compute a value;
5. a composition (`schema/templateSurface.ts`) that adds the two rules
   the build decided are NOT expressible as declaration, and wires them
   into the same finding stream.

`definition/admit.ts` is now two entry points over the engine, one per
channel. `definition/load.ts` calls it once.

The acceptance instrument was a comparison harness, since removed, whose
result is written up in `v3/implementation/p3-parity-gate.md`. The
governing decision record is `v3/adr/ADR-019-declared-schema-for-structural-definition-rules.md`;
its basis document is `v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md`.

## 2. Scope declaration — read this before deciding what counts

**What this round defends against, ratified for this round: ACCIDENT and
SLOPPINESS — a mismatch between what the declaration says and what the
engine does, a silent behaviour drift introduced by the replacement, a
regression, a guard that does not guard. Nothing else.**

A commit-holder who deliberately writes a misleading declaration owns
the diff review and this reviewer too; constructs that only a deliberate
author would produce are OUT of scope here.

**IN SCOPE.** Anything that makes the build's claims false or
unreproducible: a declared attribute the engine ignores; an engine
behaviour no declaration asks for; a rule that silently changed meaning
in the replacement; a count or claim in `p3-parity-gate.md` that does
not reproduce; a test whose fixture does not discriminate; a declared
node whose `rows` cite a contract row that does not exist or does not
say that.

**OUT OF SCOPE — record it as CARRIED-SCOPE, do not propose a fix.**
Whether the direction is a good idea; whether the vocabulary is the best
possible design; performance; naming taste; anything about surfaces this
build deliberately did not touch (the three gate-config validators under
`v3/src/gates/`, the CLI, the stdin/stdout wire contracts). Also out of
scope: proposing new machinery, new tables, or new tooling.

This bounds the *worklist*, not your *reading*.

## 3. The lenses — ordered cheapest-first, so a kill costs you least

Work them in this order and **emit findings as you go**.

**Lens A — the write-up's numbers (pure command work, do this first).**
`v3/implementation/p3-parity-gate.md` states several derived numbers.
Re-derive each and report every disagreement:
- the seven-file case count (362) and its per-file split;
- the executed-case counts it reports;
- the line-count comparison in its §5 (substrate vs. what it replaced —
  the replaced files are at `git show HEAD~3:...`);
- the claim that `pnpm v3:typecheck`, `v3:lint` and `v3:test` are green.

**Lens B — declaration vs. engine, both directions (the core of this
round).**
- Pick a spread of declared attributes across `templateFormat.ts` —
  container kinds, `presence`, `unknownMessage`, `nonempty`, `grammar`,
  `keyClass` + `keyLaneAt`, `unique` + its `at`, `memberOf` /
  `keysSubsetOf` / `disjointFrom`, `default`, `removedKeys` /
  `removedValues`, `sourceForm`, `resolvedForm`, `gating`, `channel`,
  `laneOrder`, `delegate`. For each, find where `engine.ts` consumes it
  and check the consumption matches the attribute's documented meaning.
  Report every attribute that is declared but never read, and every
  attribute read in a way its own doc comment contradicts.
- The reverse: read `engine.ts` for behaviour that NO declaration asks
  for — a hard-coded rule, a special case, a literal comparison against
  a specific key or value. The build claims it has no per-rule branch;
  a literal `if (rule === X)`-shaped construct is exactly the thing to
  hunt.

**Lens C — did the replacement change meaning?** The deleted
implementation is at `git show 9790b800:v3/src/definition/validate.ts`
and `git show 9790b800:v3/src/definition/admit.ts` (the last commit that
carries them). Pick several rules and
compare old vs. new: same condition, same path, same message text, same
issue code. The write-up claims ZERO message and ZERO path changes; each
message you find that differs is a finding. It also names the changes it
DID accept — structural rules now running on the directly-constructed
channel, and finding ORDER — so those two are not findings; anything
else is.

**Lens D — do the new tests discriminate?**
`schema/engine.test.ts` carries 31 guards, each with a MUTANT
declaration that is supposed to be the same declaration with that one
guard removed. For a sample: is the mutant really the guard removed, and
nothing else? A mutant that changes something unrelated, or that removes
a guard the fixture never exercised, makes the discrimination claim
hollow. Also check the pinned count matches the register.

**Lens E — the residual's honesty.** `schema/templateSurface.ts` names
two rules it keeps as hand-written code and gives a reason for each.
Check both reasons against the repo: is the first genuinely
inexpressible in the vocabulary, and is the second's blocker (a module
import restriction plus a port shape) real? Then look for the reverse —
a rule that IS hand-written somewhere in the changed files without being
declared or named as residual.

**Lens F — dead weight.** Any vocabulary attribute, engine branch, or
exported function with no user at all. The build removed two constructs
for this reason; report any it missed.

## 4. Evidence bar and finding format

**Every finding must carry an executed command and its real output.** A
finding stated from reading alone, however confident, is not a finding
here — this project has a recorded history of plausible review claims
that were false when run. If you could not run it, mark it `unrun`.

Emit each finding, when you confirm it, as:

```
### F<n> — <one-line title>
CLASS: IN-SCOPE | CARRIED-SCOPE | UNRUN   (one sentence on why)
WHAT:  the defect in one sentence
WHERE: file:line
EVIDENCE:
  <exact command>
  <exact output>
WHY IT MATTERS: what a reader or maintainer concludes wrongly
FIX SKETCH: (IN-SCOPE only) the smallest correction
```

## 5. Rules of engagement

- **Do not modify the repository.** Not one byte, tracked or untracked.
  A byte guard runs before and after this session; any change
  invalidates the entire verdict. Do experimental work under `/tmp`
  (copying files there to run against is fine).
- **Do not propose new machinery.** No new modules, no new attributes,
  no tooling. If you think something is missing, say so in prose.
- **Proportionality.** A correction proposal larger than the thing it
  corrects is itself a finding — say so instead of proposing it.
- **False-green is the thing to hunt.** The build's value rests on the
  declaration being the single place a structural rule lives. A rule
  that quietly lives in the engine instead, or an attribute the engine
  ignores, is the failure mode this round exists to catch.
- **Completeness over ranking.** Report everything in both classes. Do
  not silently drop small findings; do not pad with speculation.

## 6. Closing message

Only this, because everything else is already written:

- your tree's HEAD;
- counts: IN-SCOPE / CARRIED-SCOPE / UNRUN;
- which lenses you completed, which you partially ran, which you did not
  reach;
- one sentence on whether the build's central claim — every structural
  rule of this surface lives in the declaration, and the replacement
  changed no path and no message — survives what you ran.

# The parity gate — the engine-backed template surface vs. the implemented one

ADR-019 D5, run 2026-08-05 against the substrate committed at
`3466d241`. **This document is the delta list. It is presented BEFORE
any switch, never after** — that is D5's letter, and the switch is not
made until this list is ratified.

## 0. The instrument, and why it is this one

The corpus could not be replayed by "running the tests twice", because
the 362 cases carry their fixtures INSIDE `it()` blocks: a pass/fail
diff would report at test grain, and D5 asks about verdict, PATH and
MESSAGE.

So the instrument is a comparison at the call site. `schema/parityProbe.ts`
is default-OFF; with `PAIRFLOW_V3_PARITY=1` every call to `loadTemplate`'s
validate stage and to `admitTemplate` computes BOTH answers on the SAME
input and appends any divergence to a log. The reference implementation
stays the one that answers. A second switch, `PAIRFLOW_V3_ENGINE=1`,
returns the engine's answer instead — the DRY RUN of the switch, used
only to count what the switch would break.

Both variables are temporary and are removed at the switch.

## 1. What was replayed — and one correction to the corpus number

| Measure | Value | How derived, at write time |
|---|---|---|
| ADR-019 D5's corpus | 362 | `grep -cE '^\s*(it\|test)\('` over the 7 named files: 124 · 76 · 59 · 59 · 19 · 13 · 12. Reproduces exactly. |
| EXECUTED cases in those 7 files | 428 | `vitest run` on the same 7 files. The gap is loop-generated cases, which a source-literal grep cannot see. |
| EXECUTED cases actually replayed | **1830** | the whole `v3` suite |

**The corpus number under-measures the surface, and the direction
matters.** `admitTemplate` is the DIRECT-construction channel's entry,
and it is called from the testkit, the kernel, lifecycle and trace
suites — not only from the 7 named files. Restricting the replay to 362
would have hidden two thirds of the affected cases. The probe was
therefore run over the whole suite. This is a measurement refinement of
D5's corpus, not a contradiction of it: every one of the 362 is included.

Reference-path result with the probe ON: **1830 passed / 1830** — the
instrument changes no behaviour.

## 2. The headline: BOTH pre-named delta classes came back EMPTY

D5 pre-named two delta classes so they would arrive as decisions rather
than surprises. Measured over all 105 divergence records:

| Pre-named class | Measured |
|---|---|
| **path grain** (duplicate terminal ids at `terminal` not `terminal[i]`; every key-class lane at its containing map) | **0 deltas** |
| **message wording** (nine measured messages embed rule-specific rationale) | **0 deltas** |

Derived: for every finding path BOTH implementations emit, the set of
`{path, message, code}` triples at that path is identical — 0 records
with a changed message or code. The audit's §5 F3 called path grain "the
parity gate's highest-yield target"; the `at:` / `keyLaneAt:` /
`laneOrder:` attributes it recommended are why it yielded nothing.

The declaration carries a literal `message:` per lane, which is the
first of D5's two options. F5's recommendation (engine-generated wording
plus an approved delta list) was NOT taken, because taking it would have
put ~112 wording changes in front of the ratifier for no gain.

## 3. The delta list

105 divergence records, in exactly three classes.

### Class A — CHANNEL SYMMETRY: structural lanes now run on the direct-construction channel

**94 records · 4 failing cases under the dry run.**

Today the structural lanes (`ref`, `steps`, `terminal`, `roles`,
`start`, transition targets, the role-set equality, the id grammars)
exist ONLY in the file walk. `admitTemplate` never ran them, so a
directly-constructed template could be structurally broken and still be
admitted. Under ONE declaration on BOTH channels they run everywhere.

The lanes that newly fire, with occurrence counts:

| Occurrences | Path | Lane |
|---|---|---|
| 19 | `ref.version` | resolved safe-integer belt |
| 10 | `start` | `memberOf keys($.steps)` |
| 8 | `terminal` | nonempty |
| 8 | `steps` | nonempty |
| 8 | `ref.id` | grammar |
| 5 + 2 | `steps` | step-id grammar / stringness |
| 4 + 2 + 1 | `roles`, `roles.<r>` | the role-set equality, both directions |
| 4 + 3 + 1 | `steps.*.role` | role-name grammar / stringness |
| 3 | `steps.*.instruction` | nonempty |
| 3 | `steps.*.transitions` | event-type grammar |
| 3 | `runtimeContext` | spec-map required keys |
| 2 + 2 | `steps.*.transitions.<e>` | target ∈ steps ∪ terminal |
| 2 | `steps.*.gates` | dead-config subset, at a non-string key |

**This is what ADR-019 D1 asks for**, in its own words: channel symmetry
"becomes structural rather than argued", retiring the hand-partitioned
realization split that ch11-C40 and ch13-C19 each spent ratified prose
defending. The audit's F2 rests on the same mechanism: ch13-C7's entry
belt dissolves precisely because a cast-forged value can now be refused
structurally.

It is nonetheless a VERDICT change on existing cases, so it is D5's
business. The 4 failing cases are fixtures that are structurally
invalid on purpose, built to exercise something downstream:

- `admit.test.ts` — the `__proto__` own-key fixture carries `terminal: []`
  and a `start` naming no step;
- `diagEmission.test.ts` ×2 — a transition target `"vanished"` plus a
  declared-but-unused role;
- `storeCheckers.test.ts` — a transition target `"ghost"`.

Each is repairable by making the fixture structurally valid without
touching what it tests.

### Class B — LANE ORDER: the walk/admission interleaving

**11 records · 0 failing cases.**

Same finding SET, same paths, same messages, different order. Today the
walk's findings all precede admission's. Under one computation a step's
gate findings are emitted while that step is being walked, so they
interleave with later steps' structural findings. Example:

- reference: `[steps.bad container, steps.good.gates.GO[0] evaluator]`
- engine: `[steps.good.gates.GO[0] evaluator, steps.bad container]`

Finding ORDER is not named in D5 (which names verdict, path, message).
No test asserts an order that this changes. Recorded because a silent
reordering is exactly the kind of thing that should not be silent.

### Class C — the `(b′)` disposition collision on an EMPTY `steps` map

**1 record · 1 failing case.**

The ratified `(b′)` disposition (`load.test.ts`) says: an EMPTY `steps`
map plus a `round` declaration yields BOTH the C9 nonempty finding AND
the round MEMBERSHIP finding. Today it can, because two different
notions of "keys(steps)" exist: the walk's (undefined when `steps` is
empty, which suppresses `start`, transition targets and the role set)
and admission's (the actual object, against which round membership still
runs).

One engine has ONE notion. As declared, `steps`'s nonempty lane is
`gating`, so every rule selecting over `keys($.steps)` is suppressed —
including round membership. The alternative (non-gating) would make
`start` fire on an empty `steps` map, which today it does not.

**CORRECTED at the switch, 2026-08-05 — the collision was mine, not the
direction's.** The ruling received was "amend `(b′)`"; no amendment was
made, because re-reading F7's own text showed the premise of this class
was wrong. F7 states `(b′)` with its reason: *`steps` EMPTY is a valid
map OF ITS KIND — ch8-C21's suppression binds missing/wrong-kind only*,
so `keys(steps)` = ∅ EXISTS and every rule selecting over it runs.

The engine had declared `steps`'s nonempty lane `gating`, which is
suppression on an EMPTY container — precisely what ch8-C21 does not do.
Removing that one attribute restores `(b′)` exactly, with no machinery
and no second selector semantics. The ratified disposition is preserved.

The consequence, recorded because it is a real (small) widening of Class
A: with `keys($.steps)` = ∅ a reliable operand, `start` now also reports
on an empty `steps` map, which ch8-C18 requires and the old walk merely
never reached. No case in the suite objects.

Class C is therefore EMPTY. The delta list is Classes A and B.

## 4. Dry run of the switch

`PAIRFLOW_V3_ENGINE=1 vitest run`: **5 failed / 1830** at the time of the
ruling — 4 of Class A, 1 of Class C. After the Class-C correction above
and the four fixture repairs, the switch was made and the suite is
**1830 / 1830 green on the engine-backed path**.

Per-op unions (the precise kernel signatures): create → `Created \| Rejected(task_required)`; start → `Activated \| Accepted \| Duplicate \| Rejected(unknown_instance \| op_id_collision)`; kickoff → `Activated \| Duplicate \| Rejected(unknown_instance \| op_id_collision)`; cancel → `Terminated \| Duplicate \| Rejected(unknown_instance \| op_id_collision)`; fail → `Terminated \| Rejected(unknown_instance)`. HANDLE's `Outcome` type is UNTOUCHED (no new arms — the actor vocabulary is closed); the ingress `submitIntent` returns the op unions plus the `invalid_shape` rejected arm; `Started` RETIRES with the one-shot (W1). ### D5 — the parity gate

**Before the engine replaces any implemented lane, the EXISTING
fixture corpus is replayed against it and must produce identical
verdicts, identical finding PATHS and identical MESSAGES — or an
approved delta list, ratified before the switch, never after.**

The corpus, measured 2026-08-05 by `grep -cE '^\s*(it|test)\('`: **362
cases** — `validate.test.ts` 124 · `load.test.ts` 76 · `admit.test.ts`
59 · `process.test.ts` 59 · `fileDefinitionStore.test.ts` 19 ·
`threshold.test.ts` 13 · `previousReviewerVerdict.test.ts` 12.

This is a DERIVED claim about behaviour that does not exist yet; its
NAMED MEASURER is the build's parity gate itself. Two delta classes are
pre-named by the audit so they arrive as decisions rather than
surprises: **path grain** (duplicate terminal ids report at `terminal`,
not `terminal[i]`; every key-class lane reports at its containing map —
an engine written naively silently moves these addresses) and
**message wording** (nine measured messages embed rule-specific
rationale; either the declaration carries a literal `message:` or the
delta list authorizes the engine's own wording).

     1	/**
     2	 * ADR-019 (accepted 2026-08-05): the declaration VOCABULARY, as types.
     3	 *
     4	 * This module is TYPES ONLY — it defines the shape a surface declaration
     5	 * may take, and nothing else. The declaration itself is data
     6	 * (`templateFormat.ts`, D4's canonical home); the machines that consume
     7	 * it are `engine.ts` (the validator) and `normalizer.ts` (D3's second,
     8	 * separately-named capability).
     9	 *
    10	 * Every construct below is one of ADR-019 §2.2's admitted vocabulary
    11	 * entries, each of which passed the audit's ≥2-independent-ratified-rows
    12	 * test (`v3/implementation/schema-expressiveness-audit.md` §2.2, standing
    13	 * sha256 `bfed8210…`). Adding a construct is a D7 amendment of the ADR,
    14	 * never a silent edit here.
    15	 */
    16	
    17	/**
    18	 * A message template: literal text with `{placeholder}` slots the engine
    19	 * interpolates (vocabulary #18 — `message:`, the PARITY carrier; ADR-019
    20	 * D5 pre-names message wording as a delta class, and a literal template
    21	 * is the choice that keeps the measured wording byte-identical).
    22	 *
    23	 * The closed placeholder set, all supplied by the engine from the node's
    24	 * own evaluation context — never rule-specific:
    25	 *
    26	 * - `{path}`      the finding's own path
    27	 * - `{key}`       the offending key, raw
    28	 * - `{keyJson}`   the offending key, JSON-quoted
    29	 * - `{value}`     the offending value, DESCRIBED (`a map`/`a list`/literal)
    30	 * - `{valueJson}` the offending value, JSON-quoted
    31	 * - `{source}`    the offending value's raw source slice, JSON-quoted
    32	 * - `{grammar}`   the grammar's regular-expression source
    33	 * - `{members}`   the enum/allowlist members, comma-joined
    34	 * - `{keys}`      the node's legal keyset, comma-joined
    35	 * - `{label}`     the referencing site's label for a value class
    36	 * - `{ownerKey}`  the key of the nearest enclosing OPEN-map entry
    37	 */
    38	export type MessageTemplate = string;
    39	
    40	/**
    41	 * A path into the value graph, in the declaration's own address form:
    42	 * `$` root, dotted segments, `*` for "every entry of an open map", and a
    43	 * leading `..` for one level up from the citing node.
    44	 */
    45	export type DeclPath = string;
    46	
    47	/** vocabulary #12 — the selector roots. `injected` is D8's ADMITTED
    48	 * widening: a selector root that is an injected SET rather than a
    49	 * document node (a set is a set; the relation and finding form are
    50	 * unchanged). */
    51	export type Selector =
    52	  | { readonly keysOf: DeclPath }
    53	  | { readonly valuesOf: DeclPath }
    54	  | { readonly collect: DeclPath }
    55	  | { readonly union: readonly Selector[] }
    56	  | { readonly injected: "gateCatalog" };
    57	
    58	/**
    59	 * vocabulary #12 — a membership relation between the citing node's value
    60	 * (or its keys) and a selector's set.
    61	 *
    62	 * `at` is the finding's PATH GRAIN and exists because the measured lanes
    63	 * DISAGREE (audit §5 F3): the catalog-resolution lane reports at the
    64	 * BINDING, not at `…uses`. `code` is vocabulary #17.
    65	 */
    66	export interface MembershipRule {
    67	  readonly relation: "memberOf" | "keysSubsetOf" | "disjointFrom";
    68	  readonly target: Selector;
    69	  /** `self` = the citing node's own path (the default); `container` = its
    70	   * parent's path. */
    71	  readonly at?: "self" | "container";
    72	  readonly code?: string;
    73	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
    74	  /** vocabulary #13 — suppression beyond the implicit container rule. */
    75	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
    76	}
    77	
    78	/** vocabulary #11 — the duplicate lane and its path grain. */
    79	export interface UniqueRule {
    80	  readonly grain: "perOccurrence";
    81	  readonly at: "index" | "container";
    82	  readonly message: MessageTemplate;
    83	}
    84	
    85	/**
    86	 * The keyset lane ORDER. It is declared, not hard-coded, for the same
    87	 * reason `at:` is (audit §5 F3): the measured nodes DISAGREE, and an
    88	 * engine that picks one silently moves findings. Three measured orders:
    89	 *
    90	 * - `missingThenUnknown`             — the root keyset
    91	 * - `unknownThenPerKey`              — ref, round, activation, the
    92	 *                                      runtimeContext spec, every gate
    93	 *                                      config (the DEFAULT)
    94	 * - `unknownThenMissingThenValues`   — the step keyset
    95	 */
    96	export type LaneOrder =
    97	  | "missingThenUnknown"
    98	  | "unknownThenPerKey"
    99	  | "unknownThenMissingThenValues";
   100	
   101	/** Every node carries its declaration TAG (the contract's pointer target,
   102	 * D4) and the ratified rows it realizes (the tag-closure check's other
   103	 * half). `channel` marks a channel-SCOPED node: `file` for a
   104	 * SOURCE-BEARING attribute set (the engine runs it where a source exists
   105	 * and skips it where none does), `direct` for a key that exists only on
   106	 * the direct-construction channel because it belongs to the ADMITTED form
   107	 * rather than the authored one. This is D1's structural channel symmetry
   108	 * — the retired hand-partitioned "realization split". */
   109	interface NodeBase {
   110	  readonly tag: string;
   111	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   112	  readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   113	  /** vocabulary #9 — materialized ONCE at admission. Plain `default:` is
   114	   * declarable and stays schema-side (ADR-019 D3); DERIVATION is the
   115	   * normalizer's. */
   116	  readonly default?: unknown;
   117	  /** vocabulary #13 — this node's failure suppresses its dependents. */
   118	  readonly gating?: boolean;
   119	  /**
   120	   * vocabulary #2 — the keyset membership obligation, declared on the
   121	   * FIELD rather than on its container, because the measured lanes
   122	   * disagree on both grains: the template's fixed maps report a missing
   123	   * key at the CONTAINER with one shared wording, every delegated config
   124	   * schema reports it at the KEY with its own wording, and one node (a
   125	   * gate binding's `uses`) folds absence into its own type lane.
   126	   */
   127	  readonly presence?: {
   128	    readonly required: true;
   129	    readonly at?: "container" | "self";
   130	    readonly message?: MessageTemplate;
   131	    readonly code?: string;
   132	    /** Absence is reported by this node's OWN type lane, not by a
   133	     * separate missing lane (a gate binding's `uses`). */
   134	    readonly foldedIntoTypeLane?: true;
   135	  };
   136	}
   137	
   138	/** vocabulary #1 `map.fixed` — a closed keyset. The legal keyset IS
   139	 * `Object.keys(fields)`; each field declares its own `presence`. */
   140	export interface MapFixedDecl extends NodeBase {
   141	  readonly kind: "map.fixed";
   142	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   143	  /** ch8-C24's reservation: illegal today (the unknown-key lane covers
   144	   * it), named here so a future additive act finds its home and so the
   145	   * row has exactly one place to live. */
   146	  readonly reserved?: readonly string[];
   147	  /** The default missing-key wording for fields whose `presence` states
   148	   * none. */
   149	  readonly missingMessage?: MessageTemplate;
   150	  readonly unknownMessage: MessageTemplate;
   151	  readonly laneOrder?: LaneOrder;
   152	  readonly fields: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   153	  /** vocabulary #10 — fail-loud key removal with migration text. */
   154	  readonly removedKeys?: Readonly<Record<string, MessageTemplate>>;
   155	}

   157	/** vocabulary #1 `map.open` — an open key class. */
   158	export interface MapOpenDecl extends NodeBase {
   159	  readonly kind: "map.open";
   160	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   161	  /** vocabulary #3 on a map. `gating` because the measured lanes
   162	   * disagree: an EMPTY `steps` map suppresses every rule that selects
   163	   * over its keys, an empty `terminal` list does not. */
   164	  readonly nonempty?: { readonly message: MessageTemplate; readonly gating?: boolean };
   165	  /** vocabulary #5 — the open-map key class and the finding's path grain. */
   166	  readonly keyClass?: ValueClassRefDecl;
   167	  readonly keyLaneAt: "container" | "segment";
   168	  /** The gates subtree's file-channel key-STRINGNESS scan (ch11 walk /
   169	   * GP2): a DEEP scan reporting at the nearest addressable path. */
   170	  readonly deepKeyStringness?: {
   171	    readonly message: MessageTemplate;
   172	    readonly channel: "file";
   173	  };
   174	  readonly keysSubsetOf?: MembershipRule;
   175	  readonly entry: NodeDecl;
   176	}

   180	  readonly kind: "list";
   181	  readonly containerMessage: MessageTemplate;
   182	  readonly nonempty?: { readonly message: MessageTemplate; readonly gating?: boolean };
   183	  readonly member: NodeDecl;

   185	   * the list's own path (ch8-C17's measured grain). */
   186	  readonly memberLaneAt: "container" | "index";
   187	  readonly unique?: UniqueRule;
   188	  readonly disjointFrom?: MembershipRule;
   189	  readonly memberOf?: MembershipRule;

   203	/** vocabulary #6/#7 — the raw-source ladder and the value-side belt. */
   204	export interface IntegerDecl extends NodeBase {
   205	  readonly kind: "integer";
   206	  /** The alias-free / anchor-free / tag-free / `^[1-9][0-9]*$` ladder over

   212	    readonly min: number;

   219	export interface EnumDecl extends NodeBase {
   220	  readonly kind: "enum";
   221	  /** A member may be channel-SCOPED: the same `channel` attribute the node
   222	   * grain carries, applied at MEMBER grain. It is a widening, not a new
   223	   * construct — the measured token domain genuinely differs by channel
   224	   * (ch12-C1: the FILE face is authored camelCase, the direct face is the
   225	   * stored token the domain type declares). */
   226	  readonly members: readonly {
   227	    readonly value: unknown;
   228	    readonly store?: string;
   229	    readonly channel?: "both" | "file" | "direct";
   230	  }[];

   232	  readonly code?: string;

   268	/** vocabulary #16 — hand-off to an injected registration's own

   270	export interface DelegateDecl extends NodeBase {

   272	  readonly registry: "gateCatalog";
   273	  /** The sibling field whose value names the registration. */
   274	  readonly by: string;

   276	   * failure with ZERO findings must still block admission, or the binding
   277	   * would be admitted with no effective config. A property of the
   278	   * `delegate` construct, not of any one row (audit flag I3). */
   279	  readonly beltMessage: MessageTemplate;
   280	  /** vocabulary #13 — the resolution lane this hand-off presupposes. */
   281	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
   282	}

   284	export type NodeDecl =
   285	  | MapFixedDecl

   297	/**
   298	 * vocabulary #12's `equals` relation — a two-direction set equality with
   299	 * its own message per direction and its own path grain per direction
   300	 * (ch8-C16's measured form: the used-but-undeclared finding reports at
   301	 * the container, the declared-but-unused finding at the entry).
   302	 */
   303	export interface EqualsRuleDecl {
   304	  readonly tag: string;
   305	  readonly rows: readonly string[];
   306	  readonly relation: "equals";
   307	  readonly left: Selector;
   308	  readonly right: Selector;
   309	  readonly missingFromLeft: { readonly at: DeclPath; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   310	  readonly missingFromRight: { readonly at: DeclPath; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   311	  readonly dependsOn?: readonly string[];
   312	}
   313	
   314	/**
   315	 * D3 — the NORMALIZER's declared hooks. A declaration says what is legal;
   316	 * it does not compute a value, so derivation is named here and realized in
   317	 * `normalizer.ts`, never in the validator. Each hook is a named capability
   318	 * with its ratified rows; adding one is D7 format growth, not an edit.
   319	 */
   320	export type NormalizerHookDecl =
   321	  | {
   322	      readonly tag: string;
   323	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   324	      readonly hook: "expandAdvancesRound";
   325	      /** The open map of entries the flag map is expanded per. */
   326	      readonly over: DeclPath;
   327	      /** The entry's edge map, relative to the entry. */
   328	      readonly edges: string;
   329	      /** The declared advancing set. */
   330	      readonly advanceSet: DeclPath;
   331	      /** The produced sibling field. */
   332	      readonly into: string;
   333	    }
   334	  | {
   335	      readonly tag: string;
   336	      readonly rows: readonly string[];
   337	      readonly hook: "materializeEffectiveConfigs";
   338	      /** The open map of pipelines whose members carry a delegated config. */
   339	      readonly over: DeclPath;
   340	      /** The binding fields carried into the admitted form verbatim. */
   341	      readonly carry: readonly string[];
   342	      /** The produced field the effective config lands in. */
   343	      readonly into: string;
   344	    };

   346	/**
   347	 * The SUBSTRATE block (audit §2.3): how bytes become a value graph. Its
   348	 * obligations are declared here as data; the yaml library realizes them,
   349	 * which is why their WORDING is residual R6 (library-owned) rather than
   350	 * engine parity.
   351	 */
   352	export interface SubstrateDecl {
   353	  readonly read: { readonly decode: "strict-utf8"; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   354	  readonly parse: {
   355	    readonly syntax: "yaml-1.2-core";
   356	    readonly documents: 1;
   357	    readonly duplicateKeys: "reject";
   358	    readonly duplicateKeyMessage: MessageTemplate;
   359	    readonly promoteWarnings: true;
   360	    readonly directive: { readonly only: "1.2"; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   361	    readonly findingOrder: readonly ["directive", "errors", "warnings"];
   362	  };
   363	  readonly resolve: {
   364	    readonly aliases: "substrate";
   365	    readonly expansionBound: "substrate";
   366	    readonly graph: { readonly acyclic: true; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   367	  };
   368	  readonly stages: readonly ["read", "parse", "resolve", "validate", "store"];
   369	  readonly paths: { readonly root: "$"; readonly listSegment: "[i]" };
   370	  /** ch8-C23: the CLOSED issue-code namespace, disjoint from registry
   371	   * names. Declared so the engine can never mint one. */
   372	  readonly codes: readonly string[];
   373	  readonly internalFailure: { readonly path: string; readonly message: MessageTemplate };
   374	}

   382	  readonly valueClasses: Readonly<Record<string, NodeDecl>>;
   383	  readonly crossRules: readonly EqualsRuleDecl[];
   384	  readonly normalizers: readonly NormalizerHookDecl[];
   385	}

codex
### F3 — A `reserved` deklarációs attribútumnak nincs fogyasztója

CLASS: IN-SCOPE — A deklarált, de a motor által soha nem olvasott attribútumot a Lens B és Lens F kifejezetten findingként definiálja.

WHAT: A root declaration `reserved: ["kind"]` értéket hordoz, de a vizsgált forrásokban a `reserved` névnek csak a típusdefiníciója és ez az egy adat-előfordulása létezik; az engine nem olvassa.

WHERE: `v3/src/definition/schema/vocabulary.ts:146`

EVIDENCE:
```text
$ rg -n '\breserved\b' v3/src/definition/schema v3/src/definition/admit.ts v3/src/definition/load.ts
v3/src/definition/schema/templateFormat.ts:264:  reserved: ["kind"],
v3/src/definition/schema/vocabulary.ts:146:  readonly reserved?: readonly string[];
```

WHY IT MATTERS: A deklaráció olvasója joggal következtet arra, hogy a fenntartott kulcs külön, deklarált szemantikai bemenet, miközben a futás eredményére semmilyen hatása nincs; ez gyengíti az „attribútum = engine-viselkedés” központi állítást.

FIX SKETCH: A legkisebb korrekció a jelenleg hatástalan `reserved` attribútum és érték eltávolítása; a `kind` tiltását továbbra is a már működő zárt keyset/`unknownMessage` lane végzi.

 succeeded in 0ms:

    17	/**
    18	 * ADR-019 D1: THE ENGINE — one validator over declaration DATA, running
    19	 * the SAME declaration on the file-walk and direct-construction channels.
    20	 *
    21	 * The engine consumes a `SurfaceDecl`; it knows the VOCABULARY and knows
    22	 * nothing about any particular rule. A per-rule branch here (a literal
    23	 * `if (rule === X)`) is ADR-019 D9 tripwire #2 — a STOP and a scope
    24	 * review, never a work-around. Where two measured lanes disagree on a
    25	 * grain (path address, lane order, gating), the disagreement is DECLARED
    26	 * (`at:`, `laneOrder:`, `gating:`), which is the same instrument the
    27	 * audit's §5 F3 identified for path grain.
    28	 *
    29	 * Channel symmetry is structural, not argued (D1): attributes that read
    30	 * SOURCE text (`sourceForm`, the deep key-stringness scan) are declared
    31	 * once with `channel: "file"`; the engine runs them where a source exists
    32	 * and skips them where none does.
    33	 *
    34	 * This module VALIDATES and applies plain `default:` materialization. It
    35	 * does NOT derive values — derivation is the separately-named normalizer
    36	 * (D3, `normalizer.ts`).
    37	 */
    38	
    39	/** The channel the declaration is being run on. */
    40	export type EngineChannel =
    41	  | { readonly kind: "file"; readonly doc: Document; readonly source: string }
    42	  | { readonly kind: "direct" };
    43	
    44	export interface EngineOptions {
    45	  readonly channel: EngineChannel;
    46	  readonly catalog?: GateCatalog;
    47	}
    48	
    49	export interface EngineRun {
    50	  readonly findings: readonly ValidationFinding[];
    51	  /** The value with plain defaults materialized and file-channel maps
    52	   * realized as own-property records. Undefined when the root container
    53	   * lane failed (no operand exists). */
    54	  readonly normalized: unknown;
    55	  /** Binding path → the registration's EFFECTIVE config, collected as the
    56	   * `delegate` lanes resolve. The normalizer's `materializeEffectiveConfigs`
    57	   * hook consumes it. */
    58	  readonly effectiveConfigs: ReadonlyMap<string, unknown>;
    59	  /** Every binding whose resolved registration declares
    60	   * `requiresRuntimeContext` — the operand of the R3 residual cross-rule,
    61	   * which stays hand code (audit §4). */
    62	  readonly runtimeContextBindings: readonly string[];
    63	}

    65	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    66	// Value access: ONE accessor over both channels' container forms.
    67	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    68	
    69	type AnyMap = ReadonlyMap<unknown, unknown> | Record<string, unknown>;
    70	
    71	function isResolvedMap(value: unknown): value is ReadonlyMap<unknown, unknown> {
    72	  return value instanceof Map;
    73	}
    74	
    75	/** A container map on EITHER channel: the file channel's `mapAsMap` JS Map
    76	 * or the direct channel's plain object. Arrays are never maps. */
    77	function isContainer(value: unknown): value is AnyMap {
    78	  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
    79	}
    80	
    81	function entriesOf(value: AnyMap): readonly (readonly [unknown, unknown])[] {
    82	  if (isResolvedMap(value)) return [...value.entries()];
    83	  return Object.keys(value).map((key) => [key, ownGet(value, key)] as const);
    84	}
    85	
    86	function ownGet(record: Record<string, unknown>, key: string): unknown {
    87	  return Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(record, key) ? record[key] : undefined;
    88	}
    89	
    90	function containerHas(value: AnyMap, key: string): boolean {
    91	  if (isResolvedMap(value)) return value.has(key);
    92	  return Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(value, key);
    93	}
    94	
    95	function containerGet(value: AnyMap, key: string): unknown {
    96	  if (isResolvedMap(value)) return value.get(key);
    97	  return ownGet(value, key);
    98	}
    99	
   100	/** A PLAIN string-keyed map — a plain object or null-prototype object,
   101	 * never an array, a JS Map, or a class instance (`map.plain`). */
   102	function isPlainMap(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
   103	  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null || Array.isArray(value)) return false;
   104	  const proto: unknown = Object.getPrototypeOf(value);
   105	  return proto === Object.prototype || proto === null;
   106	}
   107	
   108	/** Own-property WRITE: a step id or event type legally admits `__proto__`;
   109	 * a bracket assignment would set the prototype and drop the own key. */
   110	function defineOwn<T>(target: Record<string, T>, key: string, value: T): void {
   111	  Object.defineProperty(target, key, { configurable: true, enumerable: true, value, writable: true });
   112	}
   113	
   114	/** Realize a resolved value graph as own-property records where every map
   115	 * key is a string, preserving aliased identity through one memo. A map
   116	 * with any non-string key stays a JS Map (its identity class is what the
   117	 * key lanes report on). */
   118	function materialize(value: unknown, seen: WeakMap<object, unknown>): unknown {
   119	  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null) return value;
   120	  const existing = seen.get(value);
   121	  if (existing !== undefined) return existing;
   122	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
   123	    const result: unknown[] = [];
   124	    seen.set(value, result);
   125	    for (const item of value) result.push(materialize(item, seen));
   126	    return result;
   127	  }
   128	  if (isResolvedMap(value)) {
   129	    const entries = [...value.entries()];
   130	    if (entries.every(([key]) => typeof key === "string")) {
   131	      const record: Record<string, unknown> = {};
   132	      seen.set(value, record);
   133	      for (const [key, item] of entries) defineOwn(record, key as string, materialize(item, seen));
   134	      return record;
   135	    }
   136	    const map = new Map<unknown, unknown>();
   137	    seen.set(value, map);
   138	    for (const [key, item] of entries) map.set(materialize(key, seen), materialize(item, seen));
   139	    return map;
   140	  }
   141	  return value;
   142	}
   143	
   144	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   145	// Paths and message interpolation.
   146	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   147	
   148	/** `$` is the template root; `""` is a delegated config's own root. Both
   149	 * are addressed by their children's bare key (the measured path grammar). */
   150	function joinPath(parent: string, key: string): string {
   151	  return parent === "$" || parent === "" ? key : `${parent}.${key}`;
   152	}
   153	
   154	function indexPath(parent: string, index: number): string {
   155	  return `${parent}[${String(index)}]`;
   156	}
   157	
   158	/** The finding-message value rendering: objects are DESCRIBED, never
   159	 * serialized (a cyclic graph reaches the walk). */
   160	function describeValue(value: unknown): string {
   161	  if (typeof value === "object" && value !== null) {
   162	    return Array.isArray(value) ? "a list" : "a map";
   163	  }
   164	  return JSON.stringify(value) ?? String(value);
   165	}
   166	
   167	/** A key rendered for a message: a string key verbatim, anything else
   168	 * through the same description ladder values use (a non-string key is a
   169	 * real possibility on the file channel). */
   170	function describeKey(key: unknown): string {
   171	  return typeof key === "string" ? key : describeValue(key);
   172	}
   173	
   174	interface Slots {
   175	  readonly path?: string | undefined;
   176	  readonly key?: unknown;
   177	  readonly value?: unknown;
   178	  readonly source?: string | undefined;
   179	  readonly grammar?: string | undefined;
   180	  readonly members?: string | undefined;
   181	  readonly keys?: string | undefined;
   182	  readonly label?: string | undefined;
   183	  readonly ownerKey?: string | undefined;
   184	}
   185	
   186	/** Interpolate a declared message template. The slot set is CLOSED and
   187	 * supplied by the engine from the node's own evaluation context — no slot
   188	 * carries rule-specific knowledge. */
   189	function render(template: MessageTemplate, slots: Slots): string {
   190	  return template.replace(/\{(\w+)\}/gu, (whole, name: string): string => {
   191	    switch (name) {
   192	      case "path":
   193	        return slots.path ?? whole;
   194	      case "key":
   195	        return slots.key === undefined ? whole : describeKey(slots.key);
   196	      case "keyJson":
   197	        return JSON.stringify(slots.key) ?? whole;
   198	      case "value":
   199	        return "value" in slots ? describeValue(slots.value) : whole;
   200	      case "valueRaw":
   201	        return "value" in slots ? String(slots.value) : whole;
   202	      case "valueJson":
   203	        return "value" in slots ? (JSON.stringify(slots.value) ?? String(slots.value)) : whole;
   204	      case "source":
   205	        return slots.source ?? whole;
   206	      case "grammar":
   207	        return slots.grammar ?? whole;
   208	      case "members":
   209	        return slots.members ?? whole;
   210	      case "keys":
   211	        return slots.keys ?? whole;
   212	      case "label":
   213	        return slots.label ?? whole;
   214	      case "ownerKey":
   215	        return slots.ownerKey ?? whole;
   216	      default:
   217	        return whole;
   218	    }
   219	  });
   220	}
